<a id="item-1"></a>
## [ITNet Unifies Convolution, Attention, and Recurrence](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.19538) ⭐️ 9.0/10

Researchers introduced the Integral Transform Network (ITNet), a learnable integral transform that subsumes convolution, self-attention, and recurrence as special cases, achieving state-of-the-art results across multiple benchmarks. This work simplifies neural architecture design by showing that three seemingly distinct families—CNNs, RNNs, and transformers—are instances of a single mathematical framework, potentially reducing the need for specialized architectures and enabling more flexible models. ITNet uses a learnable kernel implemented as an MLP that models pairwise interactions, and employs tiled kernel fusion, importance-weighted Monte Carlo integration, and learned low-rank factorization for efficiency. It matches or exceeds specialized baselines on ImageNet-1K, GLUE, ModelNet40, VQA v2, and NLVR2.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jun 19, 04:00

**Background**: An integral transform maps a function to another via integration, often revealing hidden properties. In deep learning, convolution, attention, and recurrence have been treated as distinct operations with different inductive biases. ITNet shows they can all be expressed as a learnable integral transform with a position- and feature-dependent kernel.

</ul>
</details>

<a id="item-2"></a>
## [LLM Personality Profiles Are Measurement Artifacts](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.20205) ⭐️ 9.0/10

A new study on arXiv shows that apparent psychological profiles of 56 instruction-tuned LLMs are largely artifacts of response bias, not stable traits, with 81-90% of between-model variance attributed to bias. This challenges the validity of using human psychological instruments on LLMs, impacting AI safety assessments and research that treats LLMs as human proxies. The study introduces 'response orthogonality' as a key predictor of instrument reliability, and shows that profiles can be manipulated by item selection.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jun 19, 04:00

**Background**: Psychological instruments like personality tests are designed for humans and assume stable traits. Response bias refers to a tendency to answer in a certain way regardless of content, which can distort measurements. Instruction-tuned LLMs are fine-tuned to follow prompts, but may exhibit systematic biases in responses.

<a id="item-3"></a>
## [Thermodynamic Measure of Intelligence Proposed](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.20231) ⭐️ 9.0/10

A new arXiv paper proposes a formal definition of intelligence as the thermodynamic amplification of rare but valid futures via recursive self-simulation, making intelligence measurable on a universal scale. This framework could fundamentally shift how intelligence is understood and measured across AI, cognitive science, and complex systems, potentially enabling a universal metric for comparing different intelligent systems. The paper defines 'thermodynamic intelligence' as rare-valid probability lift, and proves that recursive self-simulation is necessary and nearly sufficient for achieving high lift, with implications for systems from passive matter to LLMs.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jun 19, 04:00

**Background**: Intelligence has long been difficult to define formally. This work grounds intelligence in thermodynamics, treating an intelligent system as one that increases the probability of outcomes that are rare but valid under domain constraints. Recursive self-simulation arises because the system must model itself as part of the world it models.

<a id="item-4"></a>
## [DeepSeek-V4: Million-Token Context with 1.6T MoE](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.19348) ⭐️ 9.0/10

DeepSeek released DeepSeek-V4-Pro (1.6T parameters, 49B activated) and DeepSeek-V4-Flash (284B parameters, 13B activated), both supporting a one-million-token context length, using hybrid attention (CSA and HCA), Manifold-Constrained Hyper-Connections (mHC), and the Muon optimizer. This release significantly advances long-context LLM efficiency, enabling practical million-token contexts with drastically reduced compute and memory costs, which makes long-horizon tasks and test-time scaling more feasible for open models. DeepSeek-V4-Pro requires only 27% of single-token inference FLOPs and 10% of KV cache compared to DeepSeek-V3.2 in the million-token setting. The models are pre-trained on over 32T tokens and are available on Hugging Face.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jun 19, 04:00

**Background**: Mixture-of-Experts (MoE) models activate only a subset of parameters per token, enabling large total parameter counts with manageable compute. Long-context models traditionally suffer from quadratic attention cost and large KV cache; CSA and HCA compress the sequence dimension to mitigate this. The Muon optimizer improves training stability and convergence speed.

<ul>
<li><a href="https://deepseek.ai/blog/deepseek-v4-compressed-attention">DeepSeek V4 Compressed Attention: KV-Cache 2% Explained</a></li>

## [1.3B Parameter Generative Model for Chest Radiography](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.19460) ⭐️ 9.0/10

Researchers introduced the first billion-parameter generative foundation model for chest radiographs, trained from scratch on 1.2M images and 1.6T tokens, enabling controllable synthesis and editing across demographics, views, and pathologies. This model addresses poor generalization in existing radiographic AI by generating high-fidelity synthetic data, which can diversify clinical datasets and improve robustness of diagnostic models, potentially enhancing real-world clinical utility. The model uses rectified flow transformers, a class of diffusion models, and achieves synthesis fidelity indistinguishable from real radiographs to clinical experts. It supports control over demographic subgroups, acquisition views, and a dozen pathologies.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jun 19, 04:00

**Background**: Rectified flow transformers combine the efficiency of flow-based generative models with the representational power of transformers, enabling high-resolution image synthesis. Generative foundation models for medical imaging aim to produce realistic synthetic data to augment limited datasets and test diagnostic AI robustness.

## [RL Framework Trains LLMs for Long-Lifecycle Agents](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.20002) ⭐️ 9.0/10

Researchers introduced the Connect the Dots (CoD) framework, which uses end-to-end reinforcement learning to train LLMs as long-lifecycle agents that can continuously learn, explore, and adapt across tasks and environments. This work addresses a critical gap in AI agent research by enabling LLMs to develop meta-capabilities for long-term deployment, potentially leading to more autonomous and adaptable AI systems in real-world applications. The CoD framework includes a GRPO-style RL algorithm with fine-grained credit assignment and custom tasks that incentivize the targeted meta-capability, showing out-of-distribution generalization within and across domains.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jun 19, 04:00

**Background**: Long-lifecycle AI agents must solve sequences of tasks while continuously learning from experience and updating their context. Traditional LLM training often focuses on single-turn or fixed-task performance, lacking the ability to adapt over extended deployments. The CoD framework aims to instill this meta-capability through reinforcement learning with long rollout sequences that interleave task-solving and context-updating episodes.

<a id="item-7"></a>
## [NRT-Bench: Multi-Turn Red-Teaming for LLM Agent Safety](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.20408) ⭐️ 9.0/10

Researchers introduced NRT-Bench, a benchmark for evaluating LLM agent robustness under adaptive multi-turn adversarial attacks in a simulated nuclear power plant control room. The study found that adaptive attacks reliably cause safety failures, with 8.7% to 12.1% of attack sessions resulting in loss of a critical safety function. This benchmark addresses a critical gap in LLM agent safety research by providing objective harm signals and reproducible evaluation for safety-critical systems. The finding that vulnerabilities are nearly disjoint across models highlights the need for diverse safety strategies rather than relying on a single model. The benchmark uses a five-role operator team backed by LLMs, with adversaries injecting messages over four channels in bounded multi-turn sessions. Harm is measured objectively by loss of any of six critical safety functions (CSFs), not by LLM-judged text.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jun 19, 04:00

**Background**: LLM agents are increasingly proposed for supervisory roles in safety-critical systems like nuclear power plants, but their robustness under sustained adversarial pressure is poorly understood. Multi-turn red-teaming involves iterative attacks that adapt based on previous responses, simulating real-world adversaries. NRT-Bench provides a controlled environment with objective safety metrics to evaluate such threats.

## [ATProto Has No Instances: A Deep Dive](https://overreacted.io/there-are-no-instances-in-atproto/) ⭐️ 8.0/10

Dan Abramov published a blog post explaining that ATProto, the protocol behind Bluesky, does not have 'instances' like Mastodon, using analogies to RSS and email to clarify its architecture. This clarification addresses a common misconception in the decentralized social media community, helping developers and users understand the fundamental architectural differences between ATProto and ActivityPub, which could influence protocol adoption and ecosystem growth. In ATProto, Personal Data Servers (PDS) store user data, Relays aggregate data, and AppViews provide interfaces, unlike Mastodon where each instance combines all functions. This separation allows independent scaling and flexibility.

hackernews · danabramov · Jun 19, 15:10 · [Discussion](https://news.ycombinator.com/item?id=48599515)

**Background**: ATProto (Authenticated Transfer Protocol) is an open protocol for decentralized social networking, used by Bluesky. It separates data storage (PDS), data aggregation (Relays), and user interfaces (AppViews), unlike ActivityPub's server-to-server federation model where each instance is a self-contained server.

<details><summary>References</summary>
<ul>
<li><a href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/AT_Protocol">AT Protocol - Wikipedia</a></li>
<li><a href="https://atproto.brussels/atproto-architecture">ATProto Architecture • atproto .brussels</a></li>
<li><a href="https://fediversereport.com/a-conceptual-model-of-atproto-and-activitypub/">A conceptual model of ATProto and ActivityPub – The Fediverse Report</a></li>

</details>

Overall, the community engaged deeply with the technical nuances.

<a id="item-9"></a>
## [Project Valhalla Arrives in JDK 28 After a Decade](https://www.jvm-weekly.com/p/project-valhalla-explained-how-a) ⭐️ 8.0/10

Project Valhalla's value types and heap flattening are explained in detail, marking the arrival of a major JVM memory layout evolution in JDK 28 after over a decade of development. This feature significantly improves memory density and performance by eliminating object headers and indirection pointers, allowing Java to compete with lower-level languages in data-intensive applications. Heap flattening stores value objects inline in arrays without per-element headers, but only works for objects with 64-bit or smaller representations; larger objects still require indirection.

hackernews · philonoist · Jun 19, 06:35 · [Discussion](https://news.ycombinator.com/item?id=48595511)

**Background**: In traditional JVM, every object on the heap has a header (dozen bytes) and is accessed via a pointer, causing memory overhead and cache misses. Project Valhalla introduces value types (inline classes) that can be flattened into arrays, reducing memory footprint and improving locality.

<details><summary>References</summary>

<li><a href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Project_Valhalla_(Java_language)">Project Valhalla (Java language) - Wikipedia</a></li>

## [Bipartisan Bill Targets Government Jawboning of Online Speech](https://www.eff.org/deeplinks/2026/06/new-bill-takes-aim-government-pressure-silence-lawful-online-speech) ⭐️ 8.0/10

Senators Ron Wyden and Ted Cruz introduced a bipartisan bill to curb government coercion of online platforms to suppress lawful speech, a practice known as jawboning. The Electronic Frontier Foundation (EFF) has endorsed the bill. This bill addresses a growing concern over government overreach in influencing content moderation, which can chill free speech without due process. If passed, it would strengthen First Amendment protections against indirect censorship. The bill specifically targets jawboning, where the government pressures private platforms to remove content that is legally protected. It aims to increase transparency and accountability in government-platform communications.

hackernews · hn_acker · Jun 19, 17:34 · [Discussion](https://news.ycombinator.com/item?id=48600950)

**Background**: Jawboning refers to informal government pressure on private companies to censor speech, often without legal authority. Recent Supreme Court cases like Murthy v. Missouri have highlighted the constitutional concerns of such practices. The EFF has long opposed government coercion of online platforms.

This matters because public access to court records is a cornerstone of transparency and justice, and current PACER fees create a financial barrier that limits access for individuals, journalists, and researchers. The proposed legislation would replace the aging PACER and CM/ECF systems with a modern platform, aiming to improve public access, strengthen cybersecurity, and reduce long-term costs.

**Background**: PACER (Public Access to Court Electronic Records) is a system that provides online access to federal court records, but charges users per page (currently $0.10 per page, capped at $3.00 per document). Critics argue that these fees exceed the cost of running the system and have been used to fund other court technology, which a federal judge ruled illegal in 2018.

<a id="item-12"></a>
## [Amateur may have cracked Linear A using Claude Code](https://aiclambake.com/clamtakes/linear-a/) ⭐️ 8.0/10

An amateur researcher, Tom Di Mino, using Anthropic's Claude Code, claims to have deciphered the ancient Minoan script Linear A, translating over 300 words. His work is currently under academic review by linguists at Rutgers and Cambridge. Linear A has remained undeciphered for over a century, and a successful decipherment would unlock insights into Minoan civilization and early Aegean history. This also demonstrates how AI tools like Claude Code can assist in complex linguistic research by enabling systematic hypothesis testing at scale. The corpus of Linear A is extremely small—only about 7,500 characters across 1,500 inscriptions, averaging 5 characters each. Di Mino used Claude Code to build Python scripts that query and cross-reference digitized databases (GORILA and SigLA), enabling systematic testing that would be impractical manually.

hackernews · Kosturdistan · Jun 19, 16:04 · [Discussion](https://news.ycombinator.com/item?id=48600107)

**Background**: Linear A is a writing system used by the Minoans on Crete from 1800 to 1450 BC. It remains undeciphered, though its descendant Linear B was deciphered in the 1950s and found to represent an early form of Greek. The script shares many signs with Linear B, but the underlying language is unknown.

This improves security and user experience for enterprise AI tool adoption by isolating authentication flows outside the agent context, eliminating per-user consent screens and reducing context window bloat. The paper proposes AgenticRei, a deontic policy framework that extends beyond simple permit/prohibit rules to govern LLM-driven autonomous agents, covering obligations, prohibitions, dispensations, and conflict resolution at runtime. This addresses a critical gap in AI governance as current policy engines like XACML, Rego, and Cedar lack obligation lifecycle management and conflict resolution, which are essential for enterprise security and compliance in multi-agent systems. AgenticRei uses a deontic policy language built on the Rei framework, expressed in OWL and evaluated by a high-performance logic engine outside the LLM, governing both tool invocations and agent-to-agent messages via the A2AS protocol.

## [Hidden Anchors Model Multi-Agent LLM Deliberation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.19494) ⭐️ 8.0/10

A new paper models multi-agent LLM deliberation as a dynamical system where each agent has a hidden internal belief (anchor) that pulls its opinion, explaining how confidence can exceed initial beliefs. This provides a theoretical foundation for understanding why multi-agent LLM deliberation improves reasoning, with implications for AI alignment and consensus-building in AI systems. The anchor can be recovered from deliberation alone, and when it lies far from initial opinions, the system escapes the convex hull of initial beliefs, requiring a closed-loop model.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jun 19, 04:00

**Background**: Classical opinion dynamics models like DeGroot and Friedkin-Johnsen capture social influence but assume agents have no persistent internal beliefs. The convex hull of initial opinions bounds possible outcomes in those models. This paper introduces hidden anchors that allow opinions to go beyond that bound.

## [LLMs Fail to Detect Their Own Knowledge Limits on Clinical Data](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.19509) ⭐️ 8.0/10

A new study reveals that large language models (LLMs) exhibit epistemically vacuous confidence on structured clinical tabular data, outputting near-constant confidence regardless of accuracy, and proposes cross-model attribution divergence to detect epistemic blind spots. This work highlights a critical safety issue for deploying LLMs in clinical settings, where models may be confidently wrong without recognizing their own limitations, and offers a practical method to estimate reliability without accessing model internals. The study compared Qwen 2.5 7B and XGBoost on a clinical prediction task, finding that LLM verbalized confidence remained between 0.856-0.937 regardless of accuracy (49%-75.3%). Combining few-shot examples and SHAP-derived feature evidence reduced Attribution Disagreement Score from 1.54 to 0.38 and improved accuracy from 49% to 75.3% without training.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jun 19, 04:00

**Background**: Epistemic uncertainty refers to a model's lack of knowledge about the true underlying process, as opposed to aleatoric uncertainty which is inherent randomness. SHAP (SHapley Additive exPlanations) is a method to explain model predictions by assigning importance values to each feature. Cross-model attribution divergence measures how differently two models attribute importance to features, which can signal regions where one model lacks knowledge.

<a id="item-20"></a>
## [Prompt-Based Uncertainty Decomposition for LLM Agents](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.19559) ⭐️ 8.0/10

This paper proposes a simple prompt-based method to decompose LLM agent uncertainty into action confidence and request uncertainty, enabling proactive clarification seeking under black-box API constraints. It also introduces two new benchmarks, WebShop-Clarification and ALFWorld-Clarification, where 50% of tasks are deliberately underspecified. This work addresses a critical gap in LLM agent uncertainty estimation by providing a practical, training-free method that works with black-box APIs and tight latency budgets. The significant improvements in clarification F1 across multiple backbones suggest the approach can enhance agent reliability and user trust in real-world deployments. The proposed decomposition separates action confidence from request uncertainty (u), allowing the agent to ask for clarification when task specification is ambiguous. Averaged across five LLM backbones (GPT-5.1, DeepSeek-v3.2-exp, GLM-4.7, Qwen3.5-35B, GPT-OSS-120B), it improves clarification F1 on ALFWorld-Clarification by 73% over ReAct+UE and by 36% over UAM.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jun 19, 04:00

**Background**: Uncertainty quantification in LLMs typically distinguishes aleatoric (data) uncertainty from epistemic (model) uncertainty. However, recent position papers argue that this classical framework is insufficient for interactive LLM agents, which require underspecification-aware and communicable uncertainty representations to enable proactive clarification seeking and shared mental-model building. Practical deployment constraints such as black-box APIs, interactive latency budgets, and lack of labeled trajectories rule out many existing methods, making prompt-based estimation a viable alternative.

## [Narration Gap in LLM-Solver Loops](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.19588) ⭐️ 8.0/10

This paper identifies and analyzes the 'narration gap' in LLM-solver loops, where the soundness of formal solver outputs can be compromised during the narration step, and proposes certificate gating to mitigate prompt injection attacks. This work highlights a critical vulnerability in hybrid LLM-solver pipelines, which are increasingly used for safety-critical reasoning, and provides a formal framework to ensure the soundness of the final answer presented to users. The authors model the LLM-solver loop as a verified decision procedure and empirically evaluate five open-source models under prompt injection, finding that certificate gating makes the solver verdict sound, but adaptive attacks can still invert conclusions.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jun 19, 04:00

**Background**: LLM-solver loops combine large language models with formal solvers (e.g., SAT/SMT) to produce verifiable answers. The pipeline involves three steps: formalizing the question, solving it, and narrating the result. Prior work focused on the first two steps, but the narration step—where the solver's output is converted to natural language—remained unexamined, leaving a gap that can be exploited by prompt injection.

Researchers propose Evolving Programmatic Bottlenecks (EPB), the first framework to distill black-box neural combinatorial optimization (NCO) policies into human-readable program portfolios using an LLM to evolve programs. EPB addresses the critical interpretability deficit of NCO, enabling deployment and scientific diagnosis by revealing that NCO behavior shifts across optimization stages and can be approximated as compositions of classic heuristics. EPB uses a hybrid textual-numerical gradient descent scheme: numerical gradients update a student router, while textual gradients guide LLM-based program revision. It also dynamically adjusts program bank capacity via fault-targeted expansion and redundancy pruning.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jun 19, 04:00

**Background**: Neural Combinatorial Optimization (NCO) uses deep learning to solve combinatorial problems like routing, but its black-box nature hinders trust and analysis. Concept Bottleneck Models (CBMs) provide interpretability via human-defined concepts, but they are ill-suited for NCO's dynamic, state-dependent decisions. EPB overcomes this by using programs as bottlenecks, which are inherently interpretable and can capture sequential decision logic.

A new paper benchmarks agentic review systems on peer review quality and error detection, finding that OpenAIReview + GPT-5.5 achieves 83% pairwise accuracy and 71.6% recall on injected errors. This work provides the first rigorous benchmark for evaluating AI peer review systems, which are increasingly needed to handle the flood of AI-assisted research. It shows that current systems can already track human quality judgments well but have significant room for improvement in error detection. The study evaluates two open-source systems (OpenAIReview and coarse), one proprietary system (Reviewer3), and a zero-shot baseline across six LLMs. The union of detections across six models reaches 83.3% recall, suggesting different models detect different errors.

## [ICT Framework Resolves Entropy Collapse in LLM Reasoning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.19771) ⭐️ 8.0/10

Researchers propose the Independent Combinatorial Tokens (ICT) framework, which uses Jensen-Shannon divergence between token logits distributions to identify critical tokens for selective updates, preventing entropy collapse and explosion in RLVR for LLM reasoning. This work addresses a fundamental instability in RLVR optimization, offering a principled method to improve LLM reasoning quality without excessive exploration or premature convergence, with demonstrated gains of up to 14.9% on benchmarks. The ICT framework updates only the top 10% of unique tokens based on JS divergence, achieving an average pass@4 improvement of 4.58% across seven benchmarks on Qwen2.5 models (0.5B/1.5B/7B). The theoretical analysis uses both Shannon and second-order Rényi entropy to prove dual regulation of policy concentration.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jun 19, 04:00

**Background**: Reinforcement Learning with Verifiable Rewards (RLVR) trains LLMs to reason by rewarding correct answers, but suffers from entropy collapse (premature convergence) or entropy explosion (blind exploration). The Jensen-Shannon divergence measures the similarity between two probability distributions, and here it is applied to token logits to find tokens with distinctive distributional patterns.

## [AgentFinVQA: Auditable Multi-Agent Financial Chart QA](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.19782) ⭐️ 8.0/10

AgentFinVQA is a multi-agent pipeline for financial chart question answering that decomposes queries into planning, OCR, legend grounding, visual inspection, and verification steps, recording each in a traceable Model Evaluation Packet (MEP). It achieves +7.68 percentage points over a zero-shot baseline on the FinMME benchmark using a proprietary backbone, and +4.84 pp with an open-weights model served locally. This work addresses the critical need for auditability and on-premise deployment in regulated financial settings, where data privacy and trust are paramount. By demonstrating that open-weights systems can retain most accuracy gains while enabling full data residency, AgentFinVQA paves the way for practical, compliant AI in finance. The verifier's verdict serves as a confidence signal, achieving 68.2% exact accuracy on confirmed answers versus 55.6% on revised answers, enabling human-in-the-loop review routing. Error analysis reveals that question misunderstanding, legend confusion, and extraction error account for nearly two-thirds of failures and are least detected by the verifier.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jun 19, 04:00

**Background**: Financial chart question answering (QA) involves interpreting visual data like stock charts to answer queries, but regulated institutions often cannot use external APIs due to data privacy rules. Existing chart-QA agents focus on accuracy without providing audit trails or on-premise deployment options. AgentFinVQA introduces a multi-agent approach with a Model Evaluation Packet (MEP) that records each reasoning step for full traceability, addressing both auditability and deployability.

Researchers introduced ORAgentBench, a new benchmark containing 107 human-reviewed operations research tasks that require LLM agents to complete the full workflow from reading natural-language briefs and data files to writing and running solution code, with submissions validated by hidden checkers for schema, constraints, and objective quality. This benchmark fills a critical gap by evaluating LLM agents on end-to-end operations research tasks rather than isolated sub-problems, revealing that even the best agent passes only 35.51% of tasks, highlighting the need for more dependable and high-quality OR decision-making from AI agents. The benchmark includes tasks from diverse operational scenarios, each with a natural-language brief, multi-file data, configuration artifacts, and a required submission schema; agents must write and run code, and submissions are evaluated on schema validity, hard-constraint feasibility, and normalized objective quality.

NVIDIA, CMU, and UC Berkeley released ENPIRE, a harness framework that enables AI coding agents to autonomously improve real-world robot policies through a closed-loop physical feedback loop. ENPIRE addresses a key bottleneck in robotics—the heavy reliance on human supervision—by automating the entire policy improvement pipeline. This could accelerate research in dexterous manipulation and pave the way for scalable, autonomous robot learning in the physical world. The framework consists of four modules: Environment (EN) for automatic reset and verification, Policy Improvement (PI) for launching refinements, Rollout (R) for parallel policy evaluation on multiple robots, and Evolution (E) where coding agents analyze logs and improve code. The system minimizes human effort while enabling fair ablations across training recipes and agent variants.

## [RACL: Reasoning-Agent Control Layer Boosts Metaheuristic Optimization](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.20142) ⭐️ 8.0/10

RACL introduces a reasoning-agent control layer that sits above existing metaheuristic optimizers, observing and intervening in search behavior to improve performance. In vehicle routing experiments, RACL outperformed or tied baseline policies in 21 of 21 feasible cases and achieved an average cost reduction of 0.641% compared to a non-reasoning stagnation-triggered policy. This work bridges reasoning agents and metaheuristic optimization, offering a general method to dynamically control search without modifying the underlying optimizer or business constraints. It could lead to more adaptive and explainable optimization systems across logistics, scheduling, and other domains. The reasoning agent uses Codex as an in-the-loop component to observe execution logs, formulate bounded hypotheses, test interventions, and consolidate policies. The method shows no material computational overhead and is validated on vehicle routing problems, though the contribution is the control layer itself, not a specific routing solver.

**Background**: Metaheuristics are high-level problem-solving frameworks that guide lower-level heuristics to find near-optimal solutions for complex optimization problems. RACL introduces a reasoning agent that learns and applies control rules dynamically, improving adaptability and performance.

## [ModSync Prevents Capacity-Induced Failure in PINNs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.20156) ⭐️ 8.0/10

The paper identifies a capacity-induced failure mode in physics-informed neural networks (PINNs) where overparameterized networks develop task-exclusive modules, and proposes ModSync, a framework that integrates structural optimization into conflict-averse training to penalize such modularity. This work addresses a fundamental limitation in PINN training, showing that simply increasing model capacity can backfire due to functional modularity. ModSync enables robust training of larger PINNs, potentially improving accuracy in solving PDEs for scientific machine learning. ModSync penalizes task-exclusive connections while preserving interaction-promoting pathways, and achieves state-of-the-art accuracy across diverse PDE benchmarks. The code is available on GitHub.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jun 19, 04:00

**Background**: Physics-informed neural networks (PINNs) solve partial differential equations (PDEs) by embedding physical laws into neural network training. Conflict-averse optimization schemes aim to mitigate gradient interference between different loss terms, but their effectiveness degrades as model capacity increases due to functional modularity—where networks partition into task-specific subnetworks that suppress cross-objective interaction.

## [New Metric RVS Measures Logical Compliance of ML Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.20208) ⭐️ 8.0/10

Researchers introduce the Rule Violation Score (RVS), a novel metric that quantifies how well a predictive model respects a given set of logical rules, independent of predictive accuracy. In high-stakes domains like healthcare and finance, logical consistency is as critical as accuracy, yet no standard metric existed to capture it. RVS fills this gap, enabling more trustworthy model evaluation and selection. RVS distinguishes between hard rules (strict constraints) and soft rules (statistical regularities), can be applied to any predictive model over a relational vocabulary, and is computed via automatically generated SQL queries for Horn rules.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jun 19, 04:00

**Background**: Traditional ML evaluation metrics like accuracy or F1 score measure how well predictions match ground truth but ignore whether outputs violate domain-specific logical constraints. RVS addresses this by treating logical compliance as a separate dimension, crucial for applications where rule violations could have severe consequences.

SoftSkill introduces behavioral compression that converts natural-language agent skills into compact, trainable soft context prefixes, achieving significant accuracy gains on QA and math benchmarks while keeping the backbone model frozen. This approach reduces reliance on lengthy textual skill descriptions at inference time, enabling more efficient deployment of LLMs with task-specific behaviors without full fine-tuning. Using a length-32 soft prefix on Qwen3.5-4B, SoftSkill improves over no-skill prompting by 8.3 points on SearchQA, 42.1 points on LiveMath, and 1.3 points on DocVQA, and outperforms SkillOpt by 5.2 and 12.5 points on SearchQA and LiveMath respectively.

This paper identifies 'Shrinkage Bias' in non-uniform FP4 formats (E2M1) for LLM pretraining and proposes UFP4, a uniform 4-bit training recipe using E1M2/INT4 grids that avoids this bias. UFP4 consistently achieves lower loss degradation than E2M1-based methods across models up to 124B parameters, suggesting future hardware should support uniform 4-bit grids for efficient LLM training. Shrinkage Bias arises from geometric asymmetry of E2M1's representable bins and is amplified by Random Hadamard Transform (RHT); UFP4 applies RHT to all three GEMMs and restricts stochastic rounding to dY only.

FlowEdit introduces a lifelong pronunciation adaptation framework for frozen flow-matching TTS, using a Modern Hopfield Network to store and retrieve latent conditioning edits for pronunciation correction, achieving a 92.7% relative reduction in phoneme error rate on multilingual proper nouns. This enables TTS systems to correct pronunciation errors on out-of-vocabulary words without retraining, significantly improving practical deployment for real-world applications like voice assistants and audiobook narration. Corrections are stored as token-level perturbations in the text embedding space and retrieved via soft attention with a similarity gate, enabling fuzzy morphological matching. Each correction takes about 15 seconds on a single GPU.

Researchers introduced HUG, a flow-matching model that generates diverse human grasps from a single RGB-D image, using a large-scale egocentric dataset of 1 million frames of human grasping. The model enables zero-shot robot grasping by retargeting predicted grasps to various robot hands. This work bridges the gap between human dexterity and robot manipulation by leveraging natural human grasping data, significantly outperforming state-of-the-art baselines by 23% to 34% on challenging objects. It provides a scalable path for robots to grasp unseen objects in everyday environments without task-specific training. The model uses a flow-matching approach to fuse RGB and depth observations and outputs grasps parameterized by wrist translation, wrist rotation, and MANO hand pose. The dataset, 1M-HUGs, was collected using smart glasses across 41 buildings and includes 6,707 object instances, and the benchmark HUG-Bench contains 90 unseen objects with metric-scale 3D meshes.

Researchers introduced the Argent Signaling Protocol (ASP), a machine-readable header that adds structured quality signals—certainty, grounding, stochasticity, and an assumption index—to every LLM response, enabling controllers to distinguish repairable failures from containment failures in multi-agent systems. This protocol addresses a critical gap in multi-agent LLM systems: the inability to differentiate between failures that can be fixed by retrying and those that require halting, which is essential for building trustworthy AI systems. By improving pass rates and blocking ungrounded outputs, ASP enhances reliability and safety in production deployments. In standalone mode on a 27-question benchmark, ASP improved pass rate from 11.1% to 33.3% on Qwen (0.8B) and from 33.3% to 44.4% on Dobby (8B). In multi-agent mode, an ASP sidecar blocked 100% of ungrounded upstream outputs from reaching a downstream decision agent.

**Background**: Multi-agent LLM systems often suffer from semantic drift, where agents misinterpret or propagate incorrect meanings, leading to unreliable outputs. ## [Computational Identifiability: Bridging Theory and Practice](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.19361) ⭐️ 8.0/10

This paper introduces 'computational identifiability,' a new framework that defines identifiability based on finite computational search procedures rather than idealized asymptotic conditions. This distinction addresses a fundamental gap in causal inference, enabling practical identification with finite samples, ambiguous graphs, and mixed data types, which is crucial for real-world applications. The framework requires a prior distribution over parameters and a search procedure; if an estimator is found within error tolerance, identifiability is satisfied. Experiments demonstrate its use with small samples, ambiguous graphical criteria, and mixed observational-interventional data.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jun 19, 04:00

**Background**: Traditional identifiability assumes infinite data and asymptotic properties, which often fails in practice. Causal identification typically relies on causal graphs and theoretical conditions. This work proposes a computation-bound alternative that directly searches for empirical estimators.

## [Zero-Inflated Gaussians Enable Sparse EDAs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.19369) ⭐️ 8.0/10

This paper introduces multivariate zero-inflated Gaussian (ZIG) distributions as sampling laws for estimation-of-distribution algorithms (EDAs), enabling parameter-space sparsity without hand-crafted sparsity operators. This closes a long-standing gap in black-box optimization by generalizing EDAs to sparse parameter spaces, potentially improving optimization efficiency in domains like control systems and machine learning where sparse solutions are desirable. The ZIG-EDA jointly optimizes sparsity patterns and active values using a latent Gaussian model with separate indicator and value dimensions, and the latent parameters are identifiable from observed samples, unlike in missing-data settings.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jun 19, 04:00

**Background**: Estimation-of-distribution algorithms (EDAs) are evolutionary optimization methods that build and sample probabilistic models of promising solutions, avoiding hand-designed mutation and crossover operators. However, existing EDAs cannot handle sparse parameter spaces where most coefficients are zero. Zero-inflated distributions are statistical models that allow frequent zero-valued observations, making them suitable for modeling sparsity.

## [Transformer FFN Nonlinearity Is Learned, Not Fixed](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.19379) ⭐️ 8.0/10

This paper introduces a linear recoverability metric (R²_lin) to measure how nonlinear each transformer feed-forward block actually is, and finds that nonlinearity varies greatly across blocks and is a learned property, not determined by architecture or activation function. These findings challenge the common assumption that FFN blocks are uniformly nonlinear, with implications for model interpretability, compression, and efficient replacement of near-linear blocks. Across GPT-2, Pythia-160m, and Llama-160m, R²_lin ranges from >0.99 (near-linear) to <0.3 (strongly nonlinear) between adjacent blocks, and a low-rank bilinear probe recovers only a few points of R², indicating the residual nonlinearity is higher-order or distributed.

## [Wall-Clock Calibration Fixes Agent Monitor Trap](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.19386) ⭐️ 8.0/10

This paper corrects a prior finding on state saturation traps in autonomous agent monitors, revealing that the trap was an artifact of sample-time calibration and that wall-clock-calibrated monitors exhibit a different behavior with no moment-detection regime at agent cadence. This correction is significant for runtime safety monitoring of autonomous agents, as it clarifies that wall-clock-calibrated leaky-integrator monitors cannot act as moment detectors on agent streams, impacting debugging and affect modeling in long-horizon tasks. The paper shows that a wall-clock-calibrated exponential moving average (EMA) baseline produces a constant alarm at dt<=1s and silence at dt>=60s, with a critical transition zone between 1 and 30 seconds, while a sample-time CUSUM remains dt-invariant.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jun 19, 04:00

**Background**: Runtime monitors for autonomous agents often use threshold-on-state triggers based on accumulated internal states like affect models or drift statistics. Prior work reported a 'state saturation trap' where such triggers produced constant alarms on SWE-bench debugging agents, but a post-release audit revealed the engine received dt=0 between actions, invalidating the exponential decay. This paper distinguishes between sample-time calibration (e.g., CUSUM) and wall-clock-time calibration (e.g., EMA with half-lives in seconds), which behave identically on fixed-rate streams but diverge on agent streams with variable inter-action times.

## [Can In-Context Learning Drive Intrinsic Curiosity?](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.19476) ⭐️ 8.0/10

This paper investigates whether in-context learning (ICL) can replace gradient-based world models for computing learning progress rewards, enabling efficient intrinsic curiosity in exploration. It proves that unbiased ICL-based intrinsic rewards are impossible in general Markov decision processes but achievable in non-temporal settings like active learning. If successful, this approach could eliminate expensive gradient updates in curiosity-driven exploration, making intrinsic motivation scalable for large-scale reinforcement learning. It bridges in-context learning and exploration, potentially impacting autonomous data collection and scientific discovery. The paper provides both negative and positive theoretical results: unbiased ICL-based intrinsic rewards are impossible in general MDPs, but for non-temporal settings (e.g., active learning), ICL-derived rewards bound and asymptotically converge to true learning progress. Experiments in continuous and symbolic environments validate the theory.

Researchers introduced grite, an open-source, git-native coordination substrate that stores an append-only event log inside git refs, enabling multiple AI coding agents to claim and divide tasks without duplication. In experiments, grite reduced duplicate work from 78% to 0% and more than tripled useful throughput. This addresses a critical coordination gap in multi-agent coding systems, where concurrent agents often produce duplicate or conflicting work. By embedding coordination logs directly in git, grite provides a practical, decentralized solution that could significantly improve the efficiency and reliability of AI-driven software development. Grite uses an append-only event log stored in git refs (refs/grite/wal) and a materialized key-value view rebuilt from the log for fast queries. The system ensures every agent's log converges to the same state with no silent write loss, and it automatically recovers failure modes like conflicting edits, lock starvation, redundant rediscovery, and race-to-close.

A new paper reveals that pass@k underestimates difficulty on hard math problems, and shows that deterministic perturbations via activation grafting can solve many unsolved examples at matched compute. The proposed activation grafting method offers a simple diagnostic tool to recover unsolved problems, potentially improving benchmark accuracy and model evaluation. Across eight math cells (GSM8K and MATH with four models), 10.3-22.9% of examples unsolved by six sampling seeds were solved by six-chain deterministic perturbations via activation grafting, while greedy decoding alone solved at most 6%. The perturbations are mechanistically distinct, with cross-kind fix-set Jaccard ≤ 0.47 in all setups.

**Background**: Pass@k is a standard metric for evaluating LLMs on reasoning tasks, measuring the probability that at least one of k sampled solutions is correct. However, it may underestimate difficulty for hard problems because sampling can miss correct solutions that are reachable via deterministic perturbations. Activation grafting is a technique that modifies internal representations of a model by injecting activations from another input, used here as a diagnostic tool rather than a decoding method.

ToolPro introduces executable tool programs that encode multi-step service interactions with explicit effect types, replacing traditional static endpoints for LLM-based agents. It achieves up to 53.4% latency reduction and 96.1% traffic reduction in evaluations. This approach addresses a fundamental limitation in agentic web services—static endpoints poorly express complex workflows with loops, conditionals, and retries. ToolPro's programmatic interface enables more efficient and reliable agent interactions, potentially accelerating adoption of LLM-based agents in real-world applications. ToolPro combines constraint-guided program construction, effect-aware replay for exactly-once state-modifying calls, and a profile-driven policy to decide when program execution outperforms stepwise calling. It is instantiated over MCP-style services with WebAssembly sandboxing for security.

## [StreamKL: Fused GPU Kernel for Memory-Efficient KL Divergence](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.20005) ⭐️ 8.0/10

StreamKL introduces the first fused GPU primitive for computing Kullback-Leibler divergence in attention distillation, eliminating the quadratic memory cost by streaming query-key tiles through on-chip SRAM in a single forward pass. This breakthrough reduces the extra HBM footprint from O(N_Q N_K) to O(1), enabling long-context attention distillation on a single GPU, which is critical for training large language models with extended contexts. StreamKL achieves up to 43× and 14× speedups over baseline methods in forward and backward passes, respectively, and recomputes attention probabilities tile-by-tile in the backward pass to avoid storing quadratic intermediates.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jun 19, 04:00

**Background**: Attention distillation is a technique that trains one attention distribution to match another by minimizing their KL divergence, commonly used in knowledge distillation and model compression. Traditional approaches materialize both full attention matrices before computing the KL reduction, leading to O(N_Q N_K) memory and IO costs that become prohibitive for long sequences. StreamKL addresses this by deriving a novel online formulation for the coupled two-distribution KL reduction, enabling a single-pass kernel that avoids quadratic materialization.

**Tags**: `#attention distillation`, `#KL divergence`, `#GPU kernels`, `#knowledge distillation`, `#long context`

## [LLM Agents Often Over-Privilege Tool Selection](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.20023) ⭐️ 8.0/10

A new study introduces ToolPrivBench, a benchmark to evaluate over-privileged tool selection in LLM agents, and finds that mainstream agents frequently choose higher-privilege tools even when lower-privilege alternatives suffice, a problem worsened by transient failures. This work highlights a critical safety gap in LLM agents: unnecessary use of high-privilege tools can lead to privilege escalation and security breaches, and existing safety alignment fails to address it. The proposed privilege-aware defense offers a practical mitigation. ToolPrivBench covers eight domains and five recurring risk patterns, measuring both initial tool selection and escalation after transient failures. The study finds that prompt-level controls provide only limited mitigation, while a privilege-aware post-training defense substantially reduces over-privileged tool use without harming general capabilities.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jun 19, 04:00

**Background**: LLM agents often select tools from a library to perform tasks, but they may choose tools with higher privileges than necessary, violating the principle of least privilege. Prior research on tool selection focused on safety-agnostic metadata preferences, leaving privilege-sensitive choices underexplored. The principle of least privilege dictates that code should run with the lowest permissions needed.

Researchers propose Frequency-Aware Flow Matching (FAFM), which uses discrete cosine transform (DCT) to convert discrete action sequences into the frequency domain, performs flow matching on DCT coefficients, and reconstructs continuous, temporally consistent actions. ## [MIF: A Framework to Bridge the Register Gap in Nigerian Discourse](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.20255) ⭐️ 8.0/10

Researchers introduced the Meaning Intelligence Framework (MIF), a nine-dimension annotation schema for Nigerian public discourse that separates surface sentiment from true communicative intent, and identified a 'Register Gap' where zero-shot register classification accuracy was only 33.3% but rose to 73.3% with schema-informed prompting. This framework addresses a critical failure mode in NLP for African languages—context failure rather than translation failure—and could improve AI fairness and effectiveness in multilingual, high-context societies like Nigeria. ## [Robust Q-Learning for Mean-Field Control with Wasserstein Uncertainty](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.20356) ⭐️ 8.0/10

A robust Q-learning algorithm is proposed for discrete-time mean-field control problems under Wasserstein uncertainty in the common noise law, combining a quantization-and-projection scheme with a Wasserstein dual reformulation. Convergence guarantees and finite-time iteration bounds are established for both synchronous and asynchronous learning schemes, with numerical validation on systemic risk and epidemic models. This work bridges robust reinforcement learning and mean-field control, addressing model misspecification in common noise—a critical issue in multi-agent systems and financial networks. The theoretical guarantees and practical algorithm enable safer deployment of RL in large-scale stochastic systems with distributional uncertainty. The algorithm uses a quantization-and-projection scheme to discretize the common-noise space and a Wasserstein dual reformulation to handle distributional uncertainty. Numerical experiments compare asynchronous Q-learning with idealized Bellman iteration, illustrating a robustness-performance tradeoff under common-noise misspecification.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jun 19, 04:00

**Background**: Mean-field control (MFC) deals with optimal control of a large population of homogeneous agents, where the collective behavior is approximated by a mean-field term. Common noise represents uncertainty affecting all agents simultaneously, and Wasserstein uncertainty models ambiguity in the distribution of this noise. Robust Q-learning extends standard Q-learning to handle model uncertainty by optimizing against worst-case scenarios.

AutoPass is a multi-agent LLM framework that uses compiler and runtime evidence to iteratively optimize compiler performance without any training or fine-tuning. It outperforms expert-tuned heuristics and classical autotuning methods, achieving geometric-mean speedups of 1.043x and 1.117x over LLVM -O3 on x86-64 and ARM64, respectively. It bridges the gap between AI and systems research, potentially reducing the manual effort required for compiler optimization in production environments. AutoPass operates in an inference-only, training-free setting, using compiler-internal optimization states and intermediate representation (IR) to orchestrate compiler options. It is implemented on the LLVM compiler and evaluated on server-grade x86-64 and embedded ARM64 systems.

A new framework, CWE-Trace, evaluates LLMs for vulnerability detection using 834 manually curated Linux kernel samples across 74 CWEs, finding that data contamination provides no measurable advantage and that fine-tuning only calibrates outputs without improving security reasoning. This research challenges the assumption that high benchmark scores reflect genuine security understanding, revealing that current LLMs lack reliable reasoning for systems software vulnerabilities, which has critical implications for AI safety and automated code review. PCBSchemaGen is a training-free framework that uses a frozen LLM with a structured verifier and Thompson Sampling bandit to generate correct PCB schematics without unit tests or golden references, achieving 81.3% pass rate on PCBBench with Gemma-4-31B. This work addresses a critical gap in hardware design automation by enabling LLM-based code synthesis for domains lacking standard test oracles, potentially accelerating PCB design and reducing manual errors. The framework induces a domain schema from IC datasheets, uses a deterministic 5-layer continuous-reward verifier with pin-level error localization, and refines candidates via Thompson Sampling bandit. It transfers across two benchmarks with zero verifier code changes.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jun 19, 04:00

**Background**: PCB schematic design verification typically relies on manual review or SPICE simulation, but SPICE does not validate schematic-level correctness. Most LLM code-synthesis benchmarks use unit tests as reward oracles, which are unavailable for PCB schematics. PCBSchemaGen introduces a structured verifier that checks physical constraints like pin assignments and IC package compatibility.

<a id="item-126"></a>
## [Hard Constraint Diffusion via Doob's h-Transform](https://arxiv.org/abs/2602.05533) ⭐️ 8.0/10

This paper proposes a conditional diffusion guidance framework that enforces hard constraints with probability one using Doob's h-transform and martingale theory, and introduces two off-policy learning algorithms to estimate the conditioning function without modifying the pretrained score network. This work addresses a critical limitation of existing soft guidance methods by guaranteeing constraint satisfaction in safety-critical applications such as autonomous driving and medical imaging, and also enables efficient rare-event simulation for fields like finance and reliability engineering. The framework augments a pretrained diffusion model with an explicit drift correction involving the logarithmic gradient of a conditioning function, and provides non-asymptotic guarantees in total variation and Wasserstein distances. Numerical experiments demonstrate effectiveness in enforcing hard constraints and generating rare-event samples.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jun 19, 04:00

**Background**: Diffusion models generate data by reversing a noise process, but standard conditional generation methods use soft guidance that cannot guarantee hard constraints (e.g., a generated molecule must have a specific property). Doob's h-transform is a stochastic process technique that conditions a Markov process to satisfy certain events almost surely, and martingale theory provides tools to analyze such conditioned processes. This paper combines these ideas to create a principled framework for hard-constrained diffusion generation.

Researchers introduced SleepMaMi, a universal sleep foundation model that uses a hierarchical dual-encoder to capture both full-night sleep architectures and fine-grained signal morphologies from polysomnography data. ## [Decoupled Prover-Verifier Game Mitigates Legibility Tax](https://arxiv.org/abs/2602.23248) ⭐️ 8.0/10

Researchers propose a decoupled prover-verifier game (DPVG) that trains a separate translator model to convert a fixed solver's correct outputs into a checkable form, eliminating the accuracy degradation (legibility tax) seen in standard prover-verifier games. This work addresses a critical bottleneck in AI safety: ensuring highly capable LLMs produce outputs that can be verified by weaker systems without sacrificing accuracy. It offers a principled path toward scalable oversight and trustworthy AI. The DPVG framework decouples correctness from checkability by first training a solver to maximize accuracy, then training a translator to rewrite the solver's solution into a verifiable proof. The paper provides theoretical analysis showing that equilibria of DPVG correspond to faithful and checkable translators.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jun 19, 04:00

**Background**: Prover-verifier games are a technique where a 'prover' model generates solutions and a 'verifier' model checks them, aiming to improve output legibility. However, training for checkability often reduces accuracy—a phenomenon called legibility tax. This paper introduces a decoupled approach to avoid that trade-off.

## [Prompt Framing Drives Apparent Gains in Clinical VLMs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.28387) ⭐️ 8.0/10

A new study reveals that up to 70-80% of performance gains in clinical vision-language models (VLMs) on neuroimaging tasks are due to merely mentioning MRI availability in the prompt, not actual multimodal reasoning, a phenomenon termed the 'scaffold effect'. This finding exposes a critical flaw in current clinical VLM evaluations, showing that apparent multimodal gains can be artifacts of prompt framing, which undermines trust in AI for medical diagnosis and demands more rigorous evaluation protocols. The study evaluated 12 open-weight VLMs on binary classification using two neuroimaging datasets (FOR2107 and OASIS-3) where structural MRI carries no reliable individual-level diagnostic signal, and found that smaller VLMs showed gains up to 58% F1 when neuroimaging context was introduced, but this was largely due to prompt framing.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jun 19, 04:00

**Background**: Vision-language models (VLMs) are increasingly applied in clinical settings for multimodal tasks like analyzing medical images with text. However, a known issue called 'modality collapse' occurs when models rely on one modality (e.g., text) and ignore others (e.g., images). This study identifies a domain-specific form of modality collapse in clinical VLMs, where prompt framing alone drives performance gains without genuine evidence integration.

## [CogniFold: Brain-Inspired Proactive Memory for Autonomous Agents](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.13438) ⭐️ 8.0/10

Researchers introduced CogniFold, an always-on proactive memory system that autonomously organizes agent experiences into cognitive structures by extending Complementary Learning Systems (CLS) theory from two layers to three, adding a prefrontal intent layer. This work addresses a key limitation of current agent memory—its reactive, retrieval-based nature—by enabling autonomous, proactive organization of experiences, which could lead to more genuinely autonomous AI assistants. CogniFold uses graph-topology self-organization: cognitive structures proactively assemble, merge when semantically similar, decay when stale, relink via associative recall, and surface intents when concept-cluster density crosses a threshold. It was evaluated on CogEval-Bench and eight downstream benchmarks, showing robust performance.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jun 19, 04:00

**Background**: Complementary Learning Systems (CLS) theory explains how the brain uses two systems—the hippocampus for rapid encoding and the neocortex for gradual structured knowledge—for effective learning. Traditional agent memory systems are reactive and retrieval-based, lacking the ability to autonomously organize experiences into persistent structures. CogniFold extends CLS by adding a prefrontal intent layer, enabling proactive memory organization.

Science Earth introduces a planet-scale runtime and the EACN protocol that enables diverse scientific capabilities to discover and collaborate with each other without pre-defined workflows, validated in two distinct experiments. This approach could break down silos between specialized AI systems and physical instruments, enabling emergent, self-correcting scientific discovery at a global scale, potentially accelerating breakthroughs in complex fields like biology and physics. ## [OSL-MR: Learning-Augmented Memory Retention for Language Agents](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.10616) ⭐️ 8.0/10

Researchers propose OSL-MR, a learning-augmented framework that formulates memory retention in long-horizon language agents as a constrained stochastic optimization problem under partial observability, and demonstrate its effectiveness on LoCoMo and LongMemEval benchmarks. This work addresses a critical bottleneck in scaling language agents to long-horizon tasks by providing a principled, optimization-based approach to memory management, outperforming heuristic baselines and establishing a new foundation for agent memory systems. OSL-MR combines an evidence learner trained from realized evidence with a Mixed-Score heuristic that serves as an online-safe baseline and inductive prior. The paper proves the NP-hardness of the multi-step memory retention problem and shows that single-step optimization is insufficient for anticipating future demand shifts.

Existing systems treat retention locally without modeling long-term consequences under observability constraints. OSL-MR addresses this by formulating retention as a sequential decision problem with budget constraints, delayed feedback, and partial observability, using a learning-augmented approach to approximate the optimal policy.

Researchers introduced RetailBench, a simulation benchmark for evaluating LLM agents on long-horizon (180-day) retail management tasks, including pricing, replenishment, and cash-flow constraints. RetailBench models retail management as a partially observable decision process and supports thousand-day-scale simulations. ## [TerraMind: First Any-to-Any Generative EO Foundation Model](https://arxiv.org/abs/2504.11171) ⭐️ 8.0/10

Researchers introduced TerraMind, the first any-to-any generative multimodal foundation model for Earth observation, pretrained on nine geospatial modalities using dual-scale representations combining token-level and pixel-level data. TerraMind enables zero-shot and few-shot applications across diverse Earth observation tasks, and introduces 'Thinking-in-Modalities' (TiM) tuning that generates artificial data during fine-tuning and inference to improve model performance, potentially reducing the need for labeled data. The model achieves state-of-the-art performance on benchmarks like PANGAEA, and its dual-scale early fusion approach captures both high-level contextual information and fine-grained spatial nuances. The pretraining dataset, model weights, and code are open-sourced under a permissive license.

TerraMind is the first such model that can generate any output modality from any input modality, similar to how large language models generate text from text.

This unified perspective encourages deeper integration between two critical AI research areas, potentially leading to more robust and safer AI systems. The paper establishes two types of connections: (1) methods for a specific shift type can help achieve corresponding safety goals, and (2) certain shifts and safety issues can be formally reduced to each other.

RoboSSM uses Longhorn, a state-space model with closed-form updates for online associative recall, providing linear-time inference and strong extrapolation. ## [MENTOR: Flexible Teacher-Optimized Rewards for Tool-Use Distillation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2510.18383) ⭐️ 8.0/10

Researchers propose MENTOR, a reinforcement learning framework that uses a flexible teacher-optimized reward structure to distill tool-use capabilities from large language models (LLMs) to small language models (SLMs), outperforming supervised fine-tuning (SFT) on out-of-domain benchmarks. This work addresses a key limitation of SFT—poor generalization to unseen tasks—by enabling SLMs to learn flexible tool-use strategies via RL, making them more practical for real-world applications where adaptability is crucial. MENTOR uses a process-aware reward structure that balances behavioral alignment with the teacher model and downstream performance, avoiding the pitfalls of sparse rewards and strict trajectory matching. Experiments on executable-tool benchmarks show improved out-of-domain performance over SFT and strict RL baselines.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jun 19, 04:00

**Background**: Distilling tool-use capabilities from large to small language models is important for deploying efficient AI agents. Supervised fine-tuning (SFT) aligns the student model with static teacher trajectories, but this rigid alignment fails on out-of-domain tasks. Reinforcement learning (RL) offers more flexibility, but standard RL with sparse rewards provides insufficient guidance for small models, while strict trajectory matching is too restrictive. MENTOR bridges this gap by introducing a teacher-optimized reward that provides dense, process-level feedback.

## [Mitigating General-Capability Forgetting in Reasoning Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2510.21978) ⭐️ 8.0/10

A new paper reveals that reinforcement learning with verifiable rewards (RLVR) causes large reasoning models to forget foundational skills like perception and faithfulness, and proposes a replay strategy called RECAP with dynamic objective reweighting to preserve general capabilities. This work highlights a critical safety and training issue in post-training reasoning models, as forgetting general capabilities undermines model reliability and trustworthiness, and offers a practical solution that can be integrated into existing RLVR pipelines. The RECAP method uses short-horizon signals of convergence and instability to dynamically reweight training objectives, shifting focus away from saturated tasks toward underperforming or volatile ones, and experiments on Qwen2.5-VL-3B and Qwen2.5-VL-7B show it preserves general capabilities while improving reasoning.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jun 19, 04:00

**Background**: Reinforcement learning with verifiable rewards (RLVR) is a post-training paradigm that uses objective, externally verifiable signals (e.g., unit tests, formal proofs) as rewards to improve reasoning. However, prolonged RLVR training can cause catastrophic forgetting, where models lose previously learned foundational skills. Standard regularization like KL divergence only prevents deviation on the current task, not broader knowledge loss.

Researchers propose a neural network model that learns the landscape of quantum dot simulators and autotunes them toward Majorana zero modes using physics-informed unsupervised learning on synthetic conductance maps. This work could significantly accelerate the experimental realization of Majorana-based topological qubits, which are promising for fault-tolerant quantum computing. It demonstrates that AI can handle complex quantum device tuning that is otherwise time-consuming and difficult to scale. The model uses a deep vision-transformer architecture and is trained on synthetic conductance maps with a physics-informed loss that encodes properties of Majorana zero modes. A single update step can drive the system into the topological phase from a broad range of initial detunings, and iterative tuning extends the reachable parameter space.

Researchers propose the Bi-Anchor Interpolation Solver (BA-solver), which introduces a lightweight SideNet (1-2% of backbone size) to enable bidirectional temporal perception for flow matching models, achieving generation quality comparable to 100+ NFEs Euler solver in just 10 NFEs. This bridges the gap between training-free solvers (which degrade at low NFEs) and training-based acceleration (which is costly and not plug-and-play), offering a practical speedup for generative modeling without retraining the backbone. BA-solver uses two synergistic components: Bidirectional Temporal Perception via SideNet, and Bi-Anchor Velocity Integration that leverages two anchor velocities for high-order integration. It maintains high fidelity even at 5 NFEs on ImageNet-256².

BA-solver adds a tiny SideNet to the frozen backbone to predict intermediate velocities, enabling larger step sizes with minimal error.

## [PAVE: Stabilizing Q-Gradient for Smooth Actor-Critic Policies](https://arxiv.org/abs/2601.22970) ⭐️ 8.0/10

This paper theoretically proves that policy non-smoothness in actor-critic methods is governed by the critic's differential geometry, and introduces PAVE, a critic-centric regularization framework that stabilizes the Q-gradient field. This work addresses a fundamental issue in continuous control: erratic policy oscillations that hinder physical deployment. By targeting the root cause (critic geometry) rather than symptoms, PAVE offers a principled way to achieve smooth policies without sacrificing task performance. PAVE minimizes Q-gradient volatility while preserving local curvature, achieving smoothness comparable to policy-side regularization methods. The theoretical bound shows policy sensitivity is proportional to the ratio of the Q-function's mixed-partial derivative to its action-space curvature.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jun 19, 04:00

**Background**: Actor-critic methods are a class of reinforcement learning algorithms where an actor learns a policy and a critic evaluates it via a Q-function. In continuous control tasks, policies often exhibit high-frequency oscillations, making them unsuitable for real-world deployment. Previous approaches directly regularized the policy output, but this paper argues that the true cause lies in the critic's geometry.

## [DeFrame: Debiasing LLMs Against Framing Effects](https://arxiv.org/abs/2602.04306) ⭐️ 8.0/10

The paper introduces 'framing disparity' to quantify how semantically equivalent prompts with different framings (e.g., positive vs. negative wording) affect LLM fairness evaluations, and proposes a framing-aware debiasing method called DeFrame. This work reveals that existing debiasing methods fail to reduce framing-induced biases, highlighting a hidden source of unfairness in LLMs. DeFrame improves consistency across framings, which is crucial for reliable and fair LLM deployment in real-world applications. The authors augment fairness benchmarks with alternative framings and find that fairness scores vary significantly with framing. Their proposed DeFrame method uses Framing Integration and Guideline Tuning to reduce framing disparity while maintaining overall fairness.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jun 19, 04:00

Existing debiasing methods typically average fairness across frames but do not ensure consistency.

## [Flickering Multi-Armed Bandits: New Framework with Mobility Constraints](https://arxiv.org/abs/2602.17315) ⭐️ 8.0/10

This paper introduces Flickering Multi-Armed Bandits (FMAB), a novel framework that models sequential decision-making under changing action availability with mobility constraints, and proposes a two-phase lazy random walk algorithm with sublinear regret bounds and a matching lower bound. FMAB bridges the gap between classical bandit theory and real-world applications like robotics and disaster response, where agents must physically navigate to access actions, introducing a dual challenge of statistical learning and physical movement. The paper analyzes FMAB under i.i.d. Erdős–Rényi and Edge-Markovian graph processes, proving high-probability sublinear regret bounds and a matching information-theoretic lower bound, and validates the framework with a robotic disaster-response simulation.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jun 19, 04:00

**Background**: The multi-armed bandit problem is a classic reinforcement learning problem that models the exploration-exploitation tradeoff. In standard bandits, all actions are always available, but in many real-world scenarios, action availability changes over time and is constrained by the agent's physical location. FMAB extends this by modeling action availability via a stochastically evolving graph, where the agent can only move to neighboring actions.

Researchers propose RL-aware distillation (RLAD) with Trust Region Ratio Distillation (TRRD) to selectively imitate a teacher LLM during reinforcement learning, addressing distribution mismatch and objective interference in knowledge distillation for reasoning models. This work enables smaller student models to achieve strong reasoning capabilities at lower inference cost, making advanced LLM reasoning more practical for deployment. It directly tackles a key bottleneck in distilling reasoning LLMs that undergo RL post-training. TRRD replaces the traditional teacher-student KL regularizer with a PPO/GRPO-style likelihood-ratio objective anchored to a teacher–old-policy mixture, providing advantage-aware, trust-region-bounded distillation on student rollouts. Experiments on logic reasoning and math benchmarks show consistent improvements over offline distillation, standard GRPO, and KL-based on-policy distillation.

Traditional knowledge distillation methods designed for supervised fine-tuning suffer when combined with RL because the teacher's fixed traces mismatch the student's evolving rollout distribution, and the KL regularizer can conflict with reward maximization.

## [Defense Training Breaks LLM Agent Tool Use](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.19423) ⭐️ 8.0/10

A new study reveals that defense training designed to protect LLM agents from prompt injection attacks systematically destroys their tool-use competence, while failing to prevent sophisticated attacks. This capability-alignment paradox challenges current AI safety paradigms, showing that single-turn refusal benchmarks are inadequate for multi-step agents, and new approaches are needed to preserve both safety and functionality. Across 97 agent tasks and 1,000 adversarial prompts, defended models exhibited three biases: agent incompetence (immediate tool failure), cascade amplification (early errors causing 99% timeout vs 13% baseline), and trigger bias (defended models worse than undefended against simple attacks).

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jun 19, 04:00

**Background**: LLM agents use external tools like file operations and APIs to complete multi-step tasks. Prompt injection attacks manipulate agents via malicious inputs, and defense training aims to make models refuse such manipulations. However, this study shows defense training causes shortcut learning, where models overfit to surface patterns rather than understanding threats semantically.

FM-Agent is the first framework to automate Hoare-style compositional reasoning for large-scale systems using LLMs, generating function-level specifications from caller expectations and verifying code against natural-language specifications. This work addresses a key scalability bottleneck in formal verification by eliminating the need for manual specifications, enabling automated reasoning on systems up to 143k lines of code and discovering 522 previously unknown bugs in already-tested code. FM-Agent uses a top-down approach to derive function specifications from how callers expect the function to behave, and generalizes Hoare-style inference to handle natural-language specifications. It also automatically generates test cases to trigger and explain bugs.

The SocioHack sandbox includes 72 environments simulating various societal rules, such as tax codes and traffic regulations. Current LLM safeguards provide only limited mitigation against societal hacking, and the authors urge caution when collecting in-the-wild feedback for model training.

Agentra is a novel multi-agent intrusion response framework that converts security alerts into structured incident response plans, outperforming static OASIS CACAO v2.0 playbooks on a 120-event corpus by improving F1 score from 0.61 to 0.84. ## [RL Foundation Models: Synthetic MDP Priors as Next Frontier](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.18812) ⭐️ 8.0/10

A new paper argues that reinforcement learning should adopt foundation model approaches by sampling synthetic MDPs as priors, analogous to how TabPFN handles tabular data. As a proof of concept, the authors train a Graph Attention Network on synthetic MDPs that solves held-out tabular benchmarks in context without task-specific tuning. This proposal could bridge the gap between RL and the foundation model paradigm, enabling RL agents to generalize across tasks without retraining. If successful, it would dramatically reduce the computational cost and data requirements for applying RL to new problems. The paper notes that MDPs admit a fixed-size sufficient statistic independent of episode length, making them directly amenable to attention-based architectures like those used in TabPFN. The authors train a Graph Attention Network entirely on synthetic MDPs and demonstrate competitive performance both online and offline against standard algorithms like UCB-VI and Q-learning.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jun 19, 04:00

**Background**: Foundation models like GPT-4 are trained on internet-scale data, while tabular foundation models like TabPFN use synthetic data sampled from a Bayesian prior. Reinforcement learning has not yet adopted this approach; current in-context RL methods do not treat prior design as a primary objective. The paper argues that sampling synthetic MDPs is as feasible as sampling synthetic tabular datasets, and that MDPs have a tabular sufficient statistic that fits attention architectures.

Researchers introduced the TRAP benchmark to evaluate AI agents' ability to use private information for task completion while resisting active privacy extraction attacks, testing 22 models across proprietary and open-source families. This benchmark addresses a critical tension in AI agent deployment: the need to use sensitive data for tasks while preventing leakage, which is essential for safe deployment in document-intensive workflows like healthcare and finance. All tested models exhibited non-trivial privacy leakage, and instruction-following ability correlated with leakage rate; prompt-based defenses reduced leakage but at significant cost to task accuracy, and the authors proved that no soft-constraint defense can achieve both high task success and zero leakage.

Apps run in an iframe with sandbox="allow-scripts allow-forms" and an injected CSP header that blocks outbound HTTP requests, preventing data exfiltration.
