## [DIF: Denoising Implicit Feedback for Cold-Start Recommendation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.19658) ⭐️ 7.0/10

Researchers propose DIF, a model-agnostic denoising method that leverages stable user preferences via content-similar warm items to generate pseudo-labels for cold-start items, and adaptively corrects noisy labels using uncertainty estimation. The method has been deployed on Kuaishou, a billion-user short-video platform, and improved commercial metrics. Cold-start items suffer more from noisy implicit feedback (e.g., clickbait), yet existing denoising methods are ineffective for them. DIF addresses this gap with a theoretically grounded, model-agnostic approach that can be integrated into any recommender system, potentially improving recommendation quality for new items at scale. DIF infers pseudo-labels by aggregating multiple labels from content-similar warm items, weighting them by content similarity confidence. It then estimates sample-level uncertainty using relative entropy and the item's cold-start status to adaptively guide pseudo-label correction of noisy labels.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jun 19, 04:00

**Background**: Implicit feedback (e.g., clicks, views) is abundant but noisy, containing biases like clickbait and position bias. The cold-start problem occurs when new items lack interaction history, making recommendations difficult. Existing denoising methods often rely on heuristic patterns (e.g., high loss) and fail for cold items, which are more susceptible to noise.

## [Decentralized Coalition Formation via Exit-and-Join Dynamics](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.19683) ⭐️ 7.0/10

This paper models decentralized coalition formation as a dynamical process where agents unilaterally decide to exit or join coalitions based on the Aumann-Dreze value, establishing equilibrium characterizations and stability conditions. This work bridges cooperative and noncooperative game theory in multi-agent systems, offering a decentralized alternative to centralized coalition formation that could improve scalability and robustness in applications like distributed computing and autonomous teams. The dynamics use the Aumann-Dreze value to compute payoffs locally within the current coalition, and the paper identifies conditions for scalar Lyapunov or exact-potential representations, as well as the impact of switching and acceptance costs on local stability.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jun 19, 04:00

**Background**: Coalition formation is a key problem in multi-agent systems where agents group to achieve common goals. Traditional approaches often assume centralized coordination or global payoff negotiation. The Aumann-Dreze value is a solution concept for cooperative games with coalition structures that assigns payoffs based on the coalition's worth. This paper proposes a decentralized process where agents make local exit-and-join decisions, linking cooperative payoff allocation with noncooperative best-response dynamics.

## [CombEval: Dynamic Benchmark for LLM Combinatorial Counting](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.19788) ⭐️ 7.0/10

CombEval is a dynamic benchmark that evaluates combinatorial counting in large language models using typed Cofola specifications, enabling controlled generation of problems with solver-verified answers. It reveals that models remain brittle on ordered objects, indistinguishable elements, relative positional constraints, and nested dependencies. This benchmark addresses a specific reasoning gap in LLMs, providing a diagnostic testbed for combinatorial reasoning that static benchmarks cannot offer. It helps researchers understand when and why LLMs fail at counting, which is crucial for improving AI reasoning in mathematics and science. CombEval evaluates 11 LLMs under direct and code-augmented settings, using Cofola—a declarative language for combinatorial counting with Weighted First-Order Model Counting. The benchmark supports systematic variation of object type, entity scale, constraint count, and reasoning depth, with public code available on GitHub.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jun 19, 04:00

**Background**: Combinatorial counting involves determining the number of ways to arrange or select objects under constraints, a fundamental reasoning skill. Cofola is a declarative language and solver that models counting problems using Weighted First-Order Model Counting (WFOMC), allowing concise specifications and automatic solution computation. Prior static benchmarks for LLM counting lack the ability to systematically vary problem parameters, making it hard to diagnose specific failure modes.

Researchers introduced SEVRA, a serving-layer controller that selectively decides whether to verify an LLM's initial answer or accept it, reducing compute waste and harmful answer flips while maintaining accuracy. This work addresses the practical challenge of efficient LLM deployment by optimizing test-time compute allocation, showing that selective verification can save tokens and reduce regression risk without sacrificing accuracy. ## [eCNNTO: CNN Accelerates Topology Optimization with High Generalization](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.19921) ⭐️ 7.0/10

Researchers propose eCNNTO, a convolutional neural network with residual connections that predicts near-optimal densities in topology optimization, reducing iterations by up to 97% while maintaining structural connectivity. This method significantly accelerates the computationally expensive topology optimization process, enabling faster design iterations in engineering fields such as aerospace and automotive, and can generalize to unseen problems with different boundary conditions and mesh resolutions. eCNNTO builds on prior work using Deep Belief Networks but adds spatial correlations via CNNs and residual connections, and uses a novel training strategy with final-stage density histories to reduce dataset size. It achieves up to 90% iteration reduction in 2D and 97% in 3D examples.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jun 19, 04:00

Residual connections, also known as skip connections, allow gradients to flow directly through deeper networks, improving training efficiency.

This addresses a critical limitation in RL-based constrained optimization, where manual weight tuning is difficult and suboptimal, especially in dynamic environments. MAMO enables more autonomous and robust decision-making in computing and networking systems. MAMO decouples task execution from objective design by formulating weight selection as a learning problem. Researchers propose MACR, a multi-agent reasoning framework that explicitly resolves knowledge conflicts in LLMs by assessing confidence via modified semantic entropy and using three specialized agents to induce rules, analyze conflicts, and resolve inconsistencies. This work addresses a critical limitation in LLMs where conflicting parametric and contextual knowledge can lead to unreliable outputs, moving beyond binary source privileging to actively resolve conflicts, which is essential for improving LLM trustworthiness and safety. MACR first uses a modified semantic entropy measure to quantify the LLM's confidence, then either externalizes internal knowledge or retrieves external knowledge to generate basic contexts, followed by an inductive multi-agent reasoning framework with three agents for rule induction, conflict analysis, and resolution.

## [Cross-Attention Attribution for Style-Captioned TTS](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.20532) ⭐️ 7.0/10

Researchers propose cross-attention attribution for speech diffusion models, adapting the DAAM framework to the speech domain for the first time, and apply it to CapSpeech-TTS to analyze how style caption tokens influence acoustic output. This work provides the first systematic analysis of how natural language instructions shape cross-attention in speech diffusion models, enabling better interpretability and controllability for expressive text-to-speech systems. The method extracts per-token heatmaps across 25 layers and 24 ODE steps, analyzing 3,600 (style caption, text transcript) combinations. Key findings include that style tokens have lower temporal variance, attention correlates with F0 and energy, and attention entropy reaches minimum at layer 17 coinciding with style importance peak.

Hallucinations in LLMs can lead to misinformation and unreliable decisions, especially in critical applications like question answering and decision support. LUCID addresses this by incorporating KG structural information, which existing methods overlook, achieving state-of-the-art performance on nine datasets. LUCID extracts node and edge features from attention scores and semantic similarities, then integrates them with KG structure using a graph neural network. The method was evaluated on nine manually annotated benchmark datasets against 15 baselines.

Existing hallucination detection methods focus on LLM internal states or consistency with retrieved contexts, but ignore the structural information in KGs, leading to suboptimal performance.

## [ILL Linked to PGM Structure Learning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.19366) ⭐️ 7.0/10

A new paper establishes a formal connection between information lattice learning (ILL) and probabilistic graphical model (PGM) structure learning, showing that learned rules correspond to marginal constraints over interpretable abstractions. This connection provides a novel theoretical perspective that could enhance interpretable AI by grounding ILL in well-studied PGM frameworks, potentially leading to more transparent and trustworthy machine learning models. The paper demonstrates that ILL's rules are marginal laws of quotient variables induced by partitions, and that maximum-ignorance reconstruction under Shannon entropy yields a log-linear factor graph indexed by learned abstractions.

**Background**: Information lattice learning (ILL) is a cognitive modeling approach that learns interpretable rules from data by projecting onto a hierarchy of abstractions. Probabilistic graphical models (PGMs) represent joint distributions using graphs, with structure learning aiming to discover the graph from data. This paper bridges these two fields by interpreting ILL rules as constraints in a factor graph.

## [Concept Flow Models Reduce Information Leakage in CBMs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.19489) ⭐️ 7.0/10

Researchers propose Concept Flow Models (CFMs), which replace the flat concept bottleneck with a hierarchical decision tree to mitigate information leakage in concept-based reasoning. This addresses a critical flaw in Concept Bottleneck Models, where increasing concept count leads to spurious correlations, undermining interpretability. CFMs maintain predictive performance while enabling transparent, stepwise decision flows. CFMs construct decision hierarchies from visual embeddings, distribute semantic concepts at each level, and train differentiable concept weights via probabilistic tree traversal. Experiments show CFMs match flat CBM performance while reducing effective concept usage.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jun 19, 04:00

**Background**: Concept Bottleneck Models (CBMs) improve interpretability by projecting features into a human-understandable concept space, but suffer from information leakage when many concepts are used. Information leakage allows the model to exploit spurious correlations, reducing trustworthiness. Hierarchical decision trees naturally break down decisions into localized subsets, which CFMs leverage to limit leakage.

Existing methods typically use scene-level embedding, which stores high-dimensional semantic features for every 3D primitive, leading to memory and computational costs that scale linearly with scene complexity. QueryGaussian instead decouples semantic understanding from geometric representation by performing instance-level queries, avoiding the need to store dense features for the entire scene.

ParaScale introduces a gauge-invariant Parallax Number (Π) that enables faithful camera-motion transfer between videos with vastly different scales, such as a galaxy sweep versus a desk nudge. This work addresses a fundamental geometric limitation in camera-motion transfer, enabling creators to reuse cinematic moves across scenes of any scale without distortion, which could significantly impact video generation and computer vision applications. ParaScale is a plug-and-play module that reads Π from a reference video and re-realizes it against the target scene's depth per frame, leaving rotation untouched, and requires no retraining. It also introduces the Parallax Consistency Error (PCE), a scale-symmetric metric that exposes scene-scale mismatch.

**Background**: Camera-motion transfer aims to apply the motion of a reference video to a new video. A key challenge is that monocular trajectory recovery is ambiguous due to the depth-scale gauge: translation-induced image motion scales as ||T||/Z, making raw trajectories meaningless across different scales. The Parallax Number Π = ||ΔT|| / Z̄ is a dimensionless, gauge-invariant descriptor that captures how strongly a camera move is felt, independent of absolute scale.

The paper introduces Credence, a claim decomposition framework that replaces the traditional Jaccard metric with Semantic-F1, which uses BGE-large cosine similarity to better capture semantic fidelity. It also provides formal convergence theorems for repair pipelines, distinguishing between rule-based (monotone, finite) and LLM-based (non-monotone, requires early-exit) repair. This work addresses a critical limitation in automated fact-checking by providing a more accurate semantic metric for claim decomposition quality, which directly improves downstream fact-checking accuracy. The formal convergence analysis is rare in this area and provides theoretical guarantees for repair pipelines, enhancing reliability. Semantic-F1 outperforms Jaccard-F1 by +15-32 percentage points across three benchmarks (SocialClaimSplit, WikiSplitBench, ClaimDecompBench). The rule-based repair reduces Atomicity Violation Rate (AVR) by 47-100% without degrading fidelity, while LLM-based self-repair is proven non-monotone and requires an early-exit guard.

## [Adaptive Binning for Tabular Self-Supervised Learning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.19827) ⭐️ 7.0/10

Researchers propose Adaptive Binning, a training-adaptive discretization pretext for tabular self-supervised learning that progressively refines binning per feature using a coarse-to-fine curriculum. The method is accepted at MICCAI 2026 and code is available on GitHub. This work addresses a key limitation of existing binning-based pretexts that use fixed global quantiles and feature-agnostic supervision, enabling more effective self-supervised learning on medical tabular data. It has the potential to reduce reliance on costly expert labels in clinical research. Adaptive Binning uses a heterogeneity-aware objective that unifies categorical reconstruction with ordinal supervision for numerical features. It also introduces a medical tabular SSL benchmark with standardized protocols to support reproducible progress.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jun 19, 04:00

**Background**: Self-supervised learning (SSL) leverages unlabeled data by designing pretext tasks. For tabular data, binning-based pretexts discretize continuous features into bins and train models to predict bin indices, but existing methods use fixed binning that does not adapt during training. The spectral bias of neural networks describes their tendency to learn low-frequency functions first, which motivates the coarse-to-fine curriculum in this work.

## [Co-policy: Real-Time Human-Robot Music Co-Creation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.19914) ⭐️ 7.0/10

Co-policy is a framework for human-robot musical co-creation that separates semantic intent grounding, constrained musical variation, and visuomotor execution using a fine-tuned Qwen-vl planner and a Gaussian-Mixture Visuomotor Policy (GMP). This work advances embodied AI by enabling robots to generate complementary musical responses in real time, moving beyond simple playback to physically grounded co-creation with humans. The framework uses pre-inference semantic anchors and a fine-tuned Qwen-vl planner (F-Qwen) to transform speech, live musical seeds, and visual observations into structured co-creation plans, while the GMP maps target notes and visual context to multimodal robot actions in a single forward pass.

The architecture performs joint attention over audio and text tokens at a low-resolution stage for coarse alignment, then switches to alternating joint-attention and cross-attention blocks at high-resolution for detail refinement, achieving notable gains on tasks with overlapping audio events and complex instructions.

The paper introduces Boundary Embedding Shaping (BES), an adaptive contrastive learning module for GNNs that selectively suppresses spurious structural noise at decision boundaries to improve graph structural disentanglement. This work addresses a critical bottleneck in GNNs—boundary-region entanglement—which has been largely overlooked by existing robust methods. By focusing on decision boundaries, BES offers a practical plug-in module that can enhance the robustness and discriminability of GNNs in real-world graph applications. BES is a plug-in module that requires minimal parameter perturbation to the original GNN model. Experiments show consistent improvements across multiple benchmarks, with up to 5.0% improvement on WikiCS for node classification.

Existing robust GNN methods typically treat all nodes uniformly and fail to address boundary-specific vulnerabilities.

## [Controlled ML Comparison for Power System Faults](https://arxiv.org/abs/2510.00831) ⭐️ 7.0/10

This paper presents a controlled comparison of machine learning models for fault classification and localization in power systems using a common electromagnetic transient dataset and realistic 10-50 ms decision windows. The study addresses reproducibility issues in power system ML research by providing a fair benchmark, showing that nonlinear models achieve high F1 scores for fault classification and stable localization errors, which can guide practical deployment. ## [Targeted Probes Beat Universal Deception Detectors](https://arxiv.org/abs/2602.01425) ⭐️ 7.0/10

A new paper demonstrates that deception detection in AI systems requires targeted linear probes for specific deception types, as a single universal probe achieves only modest improvement (+0.032 AUC) while post-hoc oracle analysis shows potential for +0.108 AUC when probes are matched to deception types. This finding challenges the assumption that a single universal probe can reliably detect all forms of AI deception, highlighting the need for organizations to define specific threat models and deploy appropriately matched probes, which has direct implications for AI safety and alignment monitoring. The study reveals that instruction pairs capture deceptive intent rather than content-specific patterns, with prompt choice dominating probe performance (70.6% of variance). Synthetic validation experiments suggest the oracle-level performance is achievable a priori when the deception type is known in advance.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jun 19, 04:00

**Background**: Linear probes are simple linear classifiers trained on the internal activations of a neural network to detect specific concepts or behaviors. Previous work had shown that a linear probe trained on a contrastive instruction pair could detect deception, but this paper demonstrates that deception detection is inherently heterogeneous and requires targeted approaches.

## [PrototypeNAS: Zero-Shot NAS for Microcontroller DNNs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.15106) ⭐️ 7.0/10

PrototypeNAS is a zero-shot neural architecture search method that rapidly designs and specializes deep neural networks for microcontroller units by decoupling architecture design from training. It uses a three-step search process combining multiple architecture types, pruning, quantization, and an ensemble of zero-shot proxies. This work significantly reduces the time and computational cost of designing DNNs for resource-constrained MCUs, enabling rapid deployment of edge AI. It automates the specialization process, making it easier to deploy accurate models on diverse hardware without manual effort. PrototypeNAS evaluates on 12 datasets across image classification, time series classification, and object detection, identifying deployable models within minutes. It uses Hypervolume subset selection to distill Pareto-optimal architectures balancing accuracy and FLOPs.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jun 19, 04:00

**Background**: Neural architecture search (NAS) automates the design of DNN architectures but is often computationally expensive because it requires training many candidate networks. Zero-shot NAS addresses this by using training-free proxies to predict network accuracy without training. Microcontroller units (MCUs) are severely resource-constrained, making DNN deployment challenging due to limited memory and compute. PrototypeNAS combines zero-shot NAS with multi-objective optimization to rapidly generate specialized DNNs for MCUs.

## [STAR: Spatiotemporal Adaptive Reward for Text-to-Image RL](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.17979) ⭐️ 7.0/10

STAR introduces a spatiotemporal adaptive reward allocation method for RL post-training of text-to-image diffusion and flow models, using text-image attention to dynamically assign rewards to relevant latent regions across denoising steps. This addresses a key granularity mismatch in existing RL post-training, enabling more focused policy updates on generative components that affect alignment, potentially improving semantic composition, text rendering, and preference optimization without changing the reward source. STAR constructs spatial allocation maps from cross-attention inside the generative model, varying across denoising steps and rollouts, and applies stronger policy updates to more relevant regions via a spatially resolved objective. Using Stable Diffusion 3.5 Medium, it achieves scores of 0.9759 on GenEval, 0.9757 on OCR, and 23.60 on PickScore.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jun 19, 04:00

**Background**: Reinforcement learning (RL) post-training for text-to-image generation typically converts the final image reward into a single scalar advantage applied uniformly across the entire generative trajectory. However, text-to-image generation has temporal and spatial structure: different denoising steps handle different stages, and alignment-relevant content often appears only in parts of the image. This mismatch limits the effectiveness of policy updates.

Researchers propose DiverseDistill, a distillation framework that uses a committee of diverse teachers (foundation models and domain experts) with a learnable Question-Answer mechanism to improve knowledge transfer to compact domain models. This approach addresses the challenge of transferring knowledge from large foundation models to small domain-specific models, achieving up to 114% recovery of the performance gap with 38x compression, which could enable efficient deployment of powerful models in resource-constrained environments. DiverseDistill operates with frozen teachers using only forward-pass inference, requiring no parameter updates or co-training, and a dynamic teacher importance mechanism reduces training cost by filtering low-relevance teachers per sample (e.g., ~30% fewer forward passes for recommendation tasks).

**Background**: Knowledge distillation transfers knowledge from a large teacher model to a smaller student model. However, when the teacher is a large foundation model and the student is a compact domain model, the capacity gap makes distillation ineffective. Standard multi-teacher methods that naively combine heterogeneous teachers can even degrade performance. DiverseDistill introduces a learnable QA mechanism to align diverse teacher outputs into the student's representation space.

VAP uses open-vocabulary detection and embedding-based matching to ground the personal object, then injects this grounding as a visual prompt by highlighting the object and rewriting the instruction. The method is evaluated on two simulation benchmarks (Personalized-SIMPLER and Personalized-VLABench) and a real-world tabletop benchmark.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jun 19, 04:00

**Background**: Vision-Language-Action (VLA) models integrate vision, language, and action to directly output robot actions from images and text instructions. However, they struggle with personalized commands that refer to a specific instance among similar objects, as they lack instance-level grounding. VAP addresses this by treating reference images as non-parametric visual memory and using top-down selective attention.

Researchers propose PiDR, a physics-informed deep learning framework for pure inertial navigation that integrates inertial navigation principles into network training via a physics-informed residual component, achieving over 29% positioning improvement on real-world datasets from a mobile robot and an autonomous underwater vehicle. This work addresses critical limitations of black-box deep learning models in GNSS-denied environments, offering a transparent and robust solution that generalizes across different platforms and dynamics, which is essential for autonomous systems operating in adverse scenarios. Researchers introduce class-incremental motion forecasting, a new setting where autonomous vehicles must predict trajectories for new object classes over time, and propose the first end-to-end framework that adapts to new classes while preventing catastrophic forgetting. This work addresses a critical real-world limitation of current motion forecasting systems, which assume a fixed set of object classes and perfect perception, enabling autonomous vehicles to safely handle emerging object types like new vehicle models or unexpected obstacles. The framework uses an open-vocabulary segmentation model to generate 2D instance masks, then applies a 3D-to-2D keypoint voting mechanism to filter inconsistent predictions, along with a query feature variance-based replay strategy to preserve prior knowledge.

KG-SoftMAP integrates soft knowledge graph priors into Bayesian network structure learning via a MAP objective combining the BDeu score with a logit-form prior, achieving Directed-F1 scores up to 0.97 even with extremely sparse discrete data. This method addresses a critical challenge in causal discovery—learning from sparse data—by leveraging imperfect domain knowledge, which is often available in practice. It enables reliable structure recovery where data-only methods fail, with applications in education, healthcare, and other fields with limited observations. The method uses a confidence-weighted edge prior derived from a knowledge graph, which can be expert-curated or LLM-extracted. On synthetic benchmarks, KG-SoftMAP achieves Directed-F1 0.19–0.32 at observation rate ρ=0.05 and 0.44–0.97 at ρ≥0.2, while data-only learners stay near zero.

## [New Pipeline Measures CS Curriculum Alignment with Guidelines](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.19469) ⭐️ 6.0/10

Researchers propose a human-in-the-loop pipeline that uses semantic retrieval and human judgment to measure how well a computer science program covers curricular guidelines, applied to CS2013 and CS2023. This provides a reproducible, validated method for programs to assess their alignment with evolving standards, helping identify persistent gaps and adapt to raised expectations in competency and cognitive depth. The pipeline uses a reciprocal-rank-fusion ensemble as the best retriever, and found that a long-context model underperformed a small sentence model. The program covered 49.7% of CS2023 and 50.9% of CS2013 knowledge units, with competency articulation for ~88% of covered units but cognitive depth delivery dropping from 95% to 76%.

**Background**: Undergraduate computer science curricula are guided by international standards like CS2013 and CS2023, which are revised about once a decade. Programs need reliable ways to measure how well they cover these guidelines, especially as standards evolve to include competency and cognitive depth dimensions. This work introduces a human-in-the-loop pipeline that combines semantic retrieval with expert validation to produce reproducible coverage maps.

## [TOTEN: Ontology-Driven Tokenization for Physical Quantities in Brazilian Portuguese](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.19626) ⭐️ 6.0/10

Researchers propose TOTEN, a knowledge-based ontological tokenization framework that replaces statistical Byte-Pair Encoding with a formal ontology of engineering entities to handle physical quantities and technical notation in Brazilian Portuguese. This addresses a fundamental limitation of BPE tokenization, which fragments structured technical entities like '5 m/s' into arbitrary subwords, improving accuracy for domain-specific NLP tasks in engineering and scientific texts. TOTEN achieves unit ontological atomicity in all contrasts and numerical reconstruction of 0.775-0.904 on external corpora, significantly outperforming the best baseline Quantulum3 (0.627-0.703). It integrates three external oracles: Pint for dimensional analysis, Unicode Character Database for typography, and RSLP for Portuguese morphology.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jun 19, 04:00

**Background**: Byte-Pair Encoding (BPE) is a widely used tokenization algorithm in large language models that compresses text by merging frequent character pairs, but it lacks semantic understanding of structured entities like physical quantities. Ontological tokenization uses a formal ontology to classify and preserve the structure of domain-specific terms, ensuring that units, numbers, and symbols remain intact during tokenization.

OnDeFog integrates the DeFog mechanism with the Online Decision Transformer (ODT) to handle frame dropping in reinforcement learning, achieving superior performance under high drop rates and on low-reward datasets. Frame dropping is a common real-world issue in RL due to sensor failures or delays, and OnDeFog improves generalization over offline methods by learning online, making it more practical for deployment. OnDeFog is evaluated in environments with high frame dropping rates and outperforms both ODT and DeFog on datasets with abundant low-reward data. The approach builds on the Decision Transformer architecture, which reformulates RL as sequence modeling.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jun 19, 04:00

DeFog extends DT to handle random frame dropping by adding mechanisms to mitigate missing states and rewards. However, DeFog is an offline method that struggles with novel states not seen in the training data. Online Decision Transformer (ODT) enables online finetuning, and OnDeFog combines both to address frame dropping in an online setting.
